Follow-up to #17376 after reviewing the merged Vite homepage against the prior Next.js experience and profiling the real landing route.

## Findings

- Both WebGL canvases render continuously even while visually idle.
- The phone environment helper pulls in general-purpose HDR/EXR/gain-map/Draco code that this scene does not use.
- The phone model and country-flag bundle exceed the transfer work needed for the rendered result.
- The phone model is imported in the landing route instead of behind its existing Suspense boundary.
- Hydrated developer checkouts can add roughly 5 MB of unreferenced videos and product concepts to Cloudflare Pages output.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Preserve desktop/mobile visual parity and all platform-switching/onboarding interactions.
- [ ] Render the phone only when state is changing and cap the ambient shader at 30 fps.
- [ ] Honor reduced-motion without a perpetual animation loop.
- [ ] Reduce the phone GLB below 550 KB without visible geometry/UV regressions.
- [ ] Defer the phone renderer from the landing route chunk.
- [ ] Remove unused general-purpose loaders and the all-country SVG flag bundle.
- [ ] Keep Cloudflare Pages as the only deployment target.
- [ ] Pass homepage package checks, browser E2E, and root `bun run verify`.

16:Tcbe,## Project / Coordination

- Project board: [LifeOps Personal Assistant MVP — Project 15](https://github.com/orgs/elizaOS/projects/15)
- Parent tracker or epic: Related lifecycle context in #16504; this issue is limited to inactive-host producer gates and does not change renderer-service ownership.
- Coordination Discussion: N/A - the acceptance boundary is fully recorded here and in the linked draft PR.
- Suggested lane tag: `codex/lifeops-inactive-producer-gating-20260731`

## Scope

Prevent runtime producers from partially activating when their owning runtime capability is absent:

1. The agent wallet balance-delta producer must stand down before constructing its source, registering a scheduled-task dispatcher, or waiting for a runner when `ScheduledTaskRunnerService` is not active.
2. LifeOps activity-signal GET and POST routes must return an explicit 503 before rate limiting, request-body parsing, schema work, or persistence when the runtime-specific `SignalSourceRegistry` is absent.
3. Full personal-assistant plugin initialization must still register the built-in activity-signal vocabulary even when the LifeOps scheduler worker is disabled.

This is a five-file runtime-and-test slice only. It does not change UI, renderer capture lifecycle, scheduling architecture, storage schemas, model behavior, or deployment configuration.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] Scheduler-less wallet producer registration resolves without a dispatcher, source read, scheduled task, or reported runtime error.
- [ ] Inactive personal-assistant activity-signal GET and POST requests return 503 before parsing or writes.
- [ ] Inactive requests leave both `app_lifeops.life_activity_signals` and `app_lifeops.life_telemetry_events` empty and do not call `runtime.reportError`.
- [ ] Full personal-assistant initialization registers built-in signal sources independently of scheduler-worker enablement.
- [ ] The focused real-runner and real-PGlite suites, scoped Biome, diff checks, and error-policy scans pass after rebasing onto current `origin/develop`.
- [ ] Package typecheck results are reported truthfully, including any unchanged develop baseline failures.

## Evidence Plan

Keep every applicable row visible. Use `N/A - <reason>` only when a row truly does not apply.

- [ ] Screenshots or recording: N/A - no rendered UI, frontend, native, or device files change.
- [ ] Logs: Attach focused Vitest/Biome/diff command receipts and record typecheck baseline failures in the PR.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectories: N/A - no model, prompt, provider, action-selection, or response behavior changes.
- [ ] Domain artifacts: Real PGlite assertions inspect the primary activity-signal and telemetry tables and prove zero rows on the inactive path.
- [ ] CI or local commands: Run the three focused test files (25 tests), scoped Biome, `git diff --check`, patch/range equivalence, and both package typechecks.

## Claim / Status Notes

Before working, comment `CLAIMING: <scope>` with your lane tag, add this issue to the active Project, set `Status = Claimed`, and set `Claimed by = <tag>`. When active work starts, move the card to `In progress`.

Follow the full workflow in [`CONTRIBUTING.md`](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/blob/develop/CONTRIBUTING.md).
17:T8ca,## Problem

Caller-supplied container `environment_vars` are persisted in
`containers.environment_vars` and materialized through `docker create -e`.
Secret-bearing values can therefore appear in the database, the SSH command
line, Docker inspect output, and process environment.

PR #17301 attempted to replace them with `vault://` references, but its machine
credential called Steward's human `/secrets` CRUD. That surface requires an
owner/admin session plus recent MFA and intentionally rejects tenant API keys.
elizaOS also has no cloud-container resolver for `vault://cloud/env/...`; the
local vault bridge deliberately fails closed in cloud-provisioned containers.

## Required architecture

Design and ship one end-to-end workload secret path across Steward and elizaOS:

- Steward exposes a workload-scoped write/read contract which does not reveal
  tenant-wide secret inventory and does not depend on a human MFA session.
- Provisioning issues a per-container or per-agent capability with least
  privilege, explicit expiry, rotation, and revocation. A tenant-wide master
  credential must not be placed in Docker environment variables.
- The agent boot path exchanges/uses that workload identity and resolves
  references into runtime settings only. Plaintext and unresolved sentinels
  must never enter `process.env`.
- Creation, update, restart, recovery, deletion, and credential rotation have
  defined atomicity and cleanup behavior.
- The feature stays disabled until both writer and resolver are deployed and
  verified together.

## Acceptance criteria

- Real integration tests run against the actual Steward API/auth middleware,
  not an invented in-memory `/secrets` route.
- Tests cover authorization, cross-tenant denial, capability scope, expiry,
  revocation, rotation, malformed responses, partial failure, and Steward
  outage.
- Evidence proves secret values are absent from the container DB row, SSH
  command/log, `docker inspect`, application logs, and `process.env`, while the
  target plugin receives the correct runtime setting.
- Legacy/default-off behavior is explicit and the migration cannot be enabled
  before the resolver capability is present.

Supersedes the nonfunctional integration attempted in #17301.
18:Tc1c,## Problem

`Scenario PR E2E` has been failing on `develop` for over two days on a single perf assertion, and I can find no open issue tracking it. It is not a flake: every failing run I sampled fails the same way, and the measured value is 33-67% above the ceiling rather than marginally over it.

**Failing assertion** — `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/perf-interaction-kpi.spec.ts:251`, test *"scroll, sheet open/close, and drag hold a smooth frame budget"*, on the `live-token-stream` KPI asserted at line 269 via `assertFrameKpi`:

```
Error: live-token-stream: p95 66.7ms exceeds jank ceiling 50.0ms
expect(received).toBeLessThan(expected)
Expected: < 50
```

The ceiling is `P95_JANK_CEILING_MS = FRAME_BUDGET_60_MS * 3` (spec:88) — deliberately coarse, with the comment noting it exists to catch "a genuinely janky interaction (a 100ms+ p95 layout stall)" rather than to be a brittle gate. So this is the gate firing as designed, not a threshold that drifted.

**Job:** `Zero-Key app browser ratcheted` / *Actual app ratcheted keyless browser coverage* → `bun run test:e2e` exits 1.

## Observed history (develop, `Scenario PR E2E`)

| run created | p95 |
| --- | --- |
| 07-31T01:20 | 66.7 ms |
| 07-30T19:13 | 83.3 ms |
| 07-30T18:47 | 83.4 ms |
| 07-30T10:10 | 66.7 ms |
| 07-30T09:21 | 66.7 ms |
| 07-30T08:38 → 07-30T02:05 | failing (7 consecutive sampled runs) |
| 07-29T21:13 → 07-29T03:42 | failing (6 of 7 sampled; the 07-29T22:12 failure was a different assertion) |

The oldest failure I sampled is **07-29T03:42**, so the regression window opens at or before then. Two values recur exactly (66.7 ms and 83.3/83.4 ms), which looks like quantisation to a small number of dropped frames rather than noise — consistent with a real stall, and worth a look at whether the fixture is dropping a fixed number of frames.

**It predates the parent-suite merge.** #17206 merged at ~07-30T05:12; these failures start at least 07-29T03:42, so that merge is not the cause. Ruling it out here so nobody spends time bisecting it.

## Why it matters

`Scenario PR E2E` is a required lane, so develop is red and every PR inherits the failure. It also makes real regressions in that lane harder to see, because a permanently-red check trains reviewers to ignore it.

## Suggested next steps

- Bisect `develop` across the 07-28 → 07-29T03:42 window for a change touching streaming/token rendering or the chat surface's frame path.
- Confirm whether the `live-token-stream` fixture itself changed (`live-token-stream fixture: <n> tokens over <ms>` is logged at spec:289 — comparing token count and duration against an older passing run will show whether the workload grew rather than the renderer slowing).
- If the interaction genuinely got heavier for a legitimate reason, the ceiling should be re-justified explicitly rather than nudged — the spec's own comment argues for keeping it coarse.

Related but distinct, already tracked: #17325 covers the zero-key browser transcript-audio/ASR drift failing in the same workflow (`stopping transcription must POST a captured WAV to ASR`).

## Context

Supersedes the incomplete implementation in #17261.
1a:Ta8f,## Problem

The production local-inference runtime and the merged Voice Live E2E workflow disagree about what counts as supported ASR.

At current `develop` plus the native latency integration branch:

- `packages/shared/src/local-inference/voice-models.ts` declares Gemma ASR release `0.3.0` with `hfRevision: "pending"`, no downloadable assets, and missing `voice/asr/eliza-1-gemma-asr-q4_0.gguf`.
- The installed `eliza-1-2b.bundle` carries `lineage.asr.base = ggml-org/Qwen3-ASR-0.6B`.
- Production runtime checks in `asr-provenance.ts`, `engine.ts`, and `bionic-host-loader.ts` correctly reject Qwen ASR provenance for a strict/default-eligible Gemma-4 release.
- Merged PR #16970 self-provisions and directly exercises the retired Qwen compatibility assets, then reports the voice lane as genuinely real. Those direct FFI smokes bypass the product runtime provenance gate.

## Reproduction and evidence

A live iOS self-test was run with:

- real app-core API and AgentRuntime
- Cerebras `gemma-4-31b` pinned for every LLM model role
- the real fused Metal runtime and installed `eliza-1-2b.bundle`
- a real iOS simulator build/install/launch

Observed:

- host API ready in **5,039 ms**
- host log: `model mode: cerebras/gemma-4-31b; local voice bundle: eliza-1-2b; ASR ready: false`
- ASR stage: **skipped after 408 ms**, reason `local-inference ASR not ready on this host`
- SEND and TTS could not run because ASR produced no transcript
- the lane exited nonzero, as it should

The same fused runtime passes real Kokoro synthesis: 91,800 samples at 24 kHz, 3.83 s audio, first audible PCM at 769 ms, envelope CV 1.229. Its intelligibility check cannot run through the production ASR path because no verified Gemma ASR artifact exists.

## Required fix

- [ ] Publish a verified Gemma-compatible ASR model/projector pair for every supported Eliza-1 tier with immutable revision, sha256, size, and provenance.
- [ ] Replace the `pending`/empty Gemma ASR release metadata with those exact assets.
- [ ] Make Voice Live E2E provision the same artifact and enter through the production AgentRuntime HTTP route, not a direct Qwen-only FFI smoke.
- [ ] Remove or clearly relabel retired Qwen compatibility smokes so they cannot be presented as production product proof.
- [ ] Run iOS, Android, macOS Electrobun, and Windows Electrobun round trips through ASR -> live LLM -> TTS with no skipped stages.
- [ ] Attach transcripts, WER, exact per-stage latency, generated audio, runtime logs, screenshots/video, and bundle manifest provenance.
- [ ] Keep the lane red when the verified artifact is absent or rejected; no fallback, threshold waiver, or green skip.

Related: #16945, #16970, #16972, #17465.1b:T10e4,## Problem

A worker restart currently spends a job's execution-attempt budget. That is the wrong accounting: `attempts` measures how many times the *job* was tried, and a restart is an infrastructure event the job did not cause. #17258 diagnosed this correctly and was closed because its implementation did not deliver the invariant it claimed — the counter lived inside the job's payload JSON, so for `data_storage = 'r2'` it always read back zero. The large payloads most likely to pressure a worker were exactly the ones left unbounded, and an inline payload could also cross the offload threshold on rewrite and lose the counter.

An infrastructure ownership counter is not user job input and does not belong in an executor payload. It needs a database-owned column, incremented atomically under the existing recovery fence, identical for every storage mode.

## Acceptance criteria (from the #17258 review)

- [ ] a dedicated non-negative interruption/recovery counter
- [ ] incremented and failed atomically with the status transition
- [ ] ordinary `attempts` preserved
- [ ] inline and offloaded payloads covered identically
- [ ] concurrent recovery proven unable to double-increment or revive an exhausted row
- [ ] the committed-canary special case retained under its existing exact fence

## Why this ships as two PRs

`deploy-eliza-provisioning-worker.yml` restarts the daemon — which calls `recoverInProgressJobsStartedBefore` at boot — on the same paths as `cloud-cf-deploy.yml`, but **without** `needs: migrate-db`. A single PR carrying both the migration and its reader lets the daemon deploy win the race and read a column that does not exist yet: `42703`, and since both recovery loops rethrow when every job in a batch failed, recovery is dead for the length of that window.

So:

1. **PR 1** — the migration and *nothing else*, not even the Drizzle schema entry (#17476). `ADD COLUMN … integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` is metadata-only on PG11+, so it is safe on a hot table. `NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` rather than nullable because the pre-existing code writes rows without the column and must land on 0, not NULL. No `CHECK`: `ADD CONSTRAINT` full-scans under `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE`, and non-negativity is structural — the only writer is `+ 1`.
2. **PR 2** — the schema entry, the reader and the increment, once PR 1 is deployed everywhere.

**Correction to an earlier version of this split:** it originally put the Drizzle schema entry in PR 1 on the assumption that a column nothing reads is inert. It is not. Drizzle enumerates columns in every `SELECT` it generates, so the schema entry alone makes every typed read of `jobs` fail against an unmigrated database — the same race, one level up. Verified: removing the column from a test's hand-rolled `jobs` DDL while the schema entry is present yields `column "execution_interruptions" of relation "jobs" does not exist / code: 42703` on ordinary reads.

## Design notes already settled

- **The counter is never reset.** A claim always precedes an interruption, so reset-on-claim caps it at 1 and makes the bound unreachable for the poison-pill class it exists to bound. `attempts` is never reset either; new work is a new row (`reEnqueueFailedDeletions` inserts), not a recycled one.
- **Non-double-increment is carried by `status` + `execution_quiesced_at`**, which EvalPlanQual re-evaluates against the live tuple — not by `attempts`. Since this change makes `attempts` non-discriminant on the interruption arm, the counter itself must join the CAS at **both** sites (the `FOR UPDATE` select and the `UPDATE … WHERE`). Cost is nil and it makes the proof one line.
- **The committed-canary case stays deliberately unbounded** under its exact fence — it resumes idempotent cleanup without consuming a terminal attempt, which is the point.
- The canary diagnostic's `classifyTerminalFailure` has to learn the new terminal message, or `isReservedRecoveryNamespace` throws. Fail-closed, so this is a hard break rather than a degradation.
- Four hand-rolled `jobs` DDLs in tests will break with `42703` until they gain the column: `pii-scrub-jobs.test.ts` (on the hot path), `jobs-recovery.test.ts`, `container-retirement-migration.test.ts`, `container-retirement.integration.test.ts`.

Refs #17253 · replaces the closed #17258 [stan-cloud]

## Claim / Status Notes

Claim will be recorded in an immediate issue comment. No GitHub Project association is applicable to this isolated test regression.1e:Tedd,## Summary

Our PRs have been red for two days on `job exceeded the maximum execution time of 25m0s` / `The operation was canceled` — never on the diffs themselves. I went onto the runner hosts to find the cause. It is **not** what I assumed, and it is not what the agent-capacity work suggested: the self-hosted robots are oversubscribed for the CI workload, and they run on **spinning disks**.

## Measurements (live, 2026-07-31, all five prod robots)

| host | cores | busy runners | load1 | iowait | disk |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| eliza-prod-robot-2 | 12 | 8 | **47.1** | **60%** | ROTA=1 |
| eliza-prod-robot-6 | 12 | 5 | 15.8 | 5% | ROTA=1 |
| eliza-prod-robot-4 | 12 | 6 | **34.6** | 0% | ROTA=1 |
| eliza-prod-robot-3 | 12 | 5 | 8.3 | 10% | ROTA=1 |
| eliza-prod-robot-5 | 12 | 6 | 17.7 | — | ROTA=1 |

`lsblk` on robot-2: `sda`/`sdb` = **ST4000NM0024** — Seagate Constellation ES.3, 7200 rpm **mechanical**. Not SSD, not NVMe.

At the instant robot-2 was sampled: `user 5% · sys 4% · idle 31% · **iowait 60%**`, with **9 processes blocked on I/O** against 1 runnable. Top processes by CPU were `tar`, `unzstd`, `bun` — none above 4.2%. Nothing is compute-pegged; the box is waiting on platters while 8 concurrent monorepo builds extract caches and dependencies.

Loads are 0.7×–4× the core count across the fleet, and the mix shifts by phase (checkout/extract is I/O-bound, `tsc --noEmit` with an 8 GB heap and 578 Turbo tasks is CPU/RAM-bound). RAM is not the constraint: 79 GB used of 251 GB on robot-2.

## What it is NOT — two hypotheses I disproved

**1. It is not the customer agent containers.** `docker stats` on robot-2: 28 live `agent-*` containers consuming **1.1 cores of 12** in total. They are not starving CI.

**2. It is not the stuck agents from #17249 holding containers.** I cross-checked the DB against the hosts: of the 13 `deletion_failed`/`error` rows pointing at robot-2, **0 have a running container** — all 13 are pure DB rows whose container is already gone. Fleet-wide, 50 stuck rows carry a `container_name`, but on the host they do not exist. They cost capacity *accounting* (which is real — it is why an org hits its quota and why a node reads 25/24), not CPU or disk.

So the two problems are genuinely independent: #17249's agents block *quota*, the CI is slow from *runner oversubscription*.

## Consequences today

A 25-minute ceiling on `Type Check` / `Build` / `unit-tests` is not survivable when 6–8 peers share 12 cores and mechanical disks. Jobs die at the ceiling, get rerun, and the rerun joins the same contention — the queue never drains, it churns. Every red check on our open PRs this week traces to this, not to a diff.

## Options, with the tradeoff stated

1. **Lower runners per host** (6–8 → 3–4). Directly halves contention. Costs queue depth, but a job that finishes in 30 min beats a job that dies at 25 and is rerun twice. Cheapest lever, reversible, no hardware.
2. **Move the heavy lanes off the HDD robots** (typecheck/build/e2e to SSD-backed or hosted runners; keep light lanes on the robots).
3. **Give the robots SSDs** — the real fix for a monorepo CI, and the only one that addresses `tar`/`unzstd` cache extraction at the root.
4. Raising the 25-minute ceiling would only convert timeouts into long queues; it treats the symptom.

I have SSH on these hosts but will not change runner concurrency on shared CI infrastructure unilaterally — it trades everyone's throughput. Happy to apply option 1 on one host as a measured experiment if a maintainer says go, with before/after load and job-duration numbers.

Related: #17157 (stale runner workspaces — real but secondary here: on robot-2 the two large `_work` trees are today's active checkouts, and two runners are at 0 bytes). [stan-cloud]
1f:T84b,## Problem

The open group-room coordination PR does not yet establish a production-safe distributed delivery contract. Trust is self-published from runtime settings, lease expiry uses process clocks, recovery can leave work stranded, and one slot-level delivery marker cannot represent partial multi-chunk Discord sends. Its evidence also overstates exactly-once and RLS guarantees.

## Scope

Replace the coordination path with a durable SQL state machine that:

- derives contender authority from an authenticated Discord guild/account boundary;
- uses database time and atomic compare-and-set transitions for claims, renewal, recovery, release, and terminal delivery;
- separates the one human reply lane from the per-human-edge bot reply budget;
- stores and reconciles every outbound chunk with deterministic Discord nonce + `enforce_nonce`;
- makes crash recovery bounded, account-affine, and non-stranding on every error path;
- reports ambiguous delivery and diagnostic failures instead of fabricating success;
- remains default-off and fails closed if durable SQL/schema support is unavailable;
- is covered by real plugin-sql/PGLite concurrency, rollback, recovery, and connector-boundary tests, plus PostgreSQL RLS verification when a real Postgres service is available.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] two independent runtimes cannot both claim the human lane
- [ ] bot messages never advance the human edge and human replies never consume bot budget
- [ ] forged/unattested scope is rejected before SQL mutation
- [ ] stale workers cannot send or reconcile after recovery transfers ownership
- [ ] partial chunk delivery resumes/reconciles per chunk without regenerating delivered chunks
- [ ] all recovery paths end in a durable retry, delivery, explicit abandonment, or observable ambiguous state
- [ ] PGLite integration tests exercise real transactions, concurrency, rollback, and restart state
- [ ] connector tests exercise authenticated guild/account derivation and bot-vs-human behavior
- [ ] no claim of exactly-once delivery; evidence states Discord nonce guarantees and residual ambiguity precisely

## Evidence required

Focused core execution/budget/purity tests, affected provider tests, core and agent typechecks/builds, and current-base CI.24:Tb25,## Problem

elizaOS agents can already chat, browse, code, and hold crypto — but they can't autonomously pay for premium APIs. If an agent needs a captcha solved, a web page scraped, a token security scan, or a Polymarket odds lookup, a human has to approve every $0.01 transaction. That breaks the "autonomous agent OS" vision.

## What x402 is

[x402](https://x402.org) is an open M2M payment protocol built on HTTP `402 Payment Required`. An API returns `402` with a USDC invoice; the agent client pays it automatically via a crypto wallet, then retries and gets the result. No API keys, no monthly subscriptions, no human in the loop.

[minia2a.uk](https://minia2a.uk) is a live marketplace with 174+ pay-per-call APIs — captcha solving, web scraping, token security, Polymarket data, wallet intel, email verification, and 80+ more. 115 agents already registered.

## Why it fits elizaOS

1. **Plugin architecture already exists** — Actions, Providers, Services, Evaluators. An `@elizaos/plugin-x402` would be a Service that intercepts HTTP calls and handles 402 responses transparently.

2. **Wallets are already built in** — non-custodial EVM + Solana. x402 needs exactly this to sign and send USDC payments.

3. **Eliza Cloud monetization** — the repo already talks about "metering and creator earnings for published apps, agents, and MCPs." x402 is the protocol-level version of that: any API author can meter and charge, not just Eliza-hosted ones.

4. **TypeScript-native** — the existing x402 client SDKs (agent402-openai-agents, agent402-langchain) are Python. A TypeScript `@elizaos/plugin-x402` would be the first JS/TS SDK in the ecosystem, giving elizaOS a first-mover advantage.

## Sketch

```typescript
// eliza.yml or plugin config
plugins:
  - name: "@elizaos/plugin-x402"
    config:
      wallet: "0x..."      // or reuse built-in wallet
      maxPerCall: "1.00"   // USDC spending cap
      dailyLimit: "10.00"

// After plugin loads, any HTTP call that gets a 402:
//   → plugin intercepts
//   → checks spending limits  
//   → signs + sends USDC tx on Base
//   → retries original request
//   → agent gets the result without human approval
```

## Prior art

- [agent402-openai-agents](https://github.com/minia2a/agent402-openai-agents) — x402 middleware for OpenAI Agents SDK
- [agent402-langchain](https://github.com/minia2a/agent402-langchain) — x402 tool wrapper for LangChain
- [awesome-x402](https://minia2a.uk/awesome-x402.md) — ecosystem overview

## Who would use this

Any elizaOS agent that calls external APIs — which is basically all of them. The first agent framework with built-in autonomous payments has a real competitive edge in the M2M economy.

Happy to help with implementation or answer questions. 🤖

Merged PR #17442 correctly makes job-failure and dependent-row writes atomic, but its recovery boundary still has two operational defects:

1. A failed post-commit app-cache invalidation is caught, labeled J7, logged, and forgotten after the job is terminal. The job will never be selected by recovery again, so the deploy-status route can continue serving `building` until TTL. Cache invalidation is not diagnostics and J7 does not apply.
2. Recovery throws when all rows in one per-type batch fail. `ProvisioningJobService` loops types sequentially, so one poisoned singleton row aborts every later type on every cycle. The apps worker catches at the process boundary but then repeats the same starvation.

The repository catches also return an undifferentiated `number`, erasing partial failures contrary to the fail-fast/error-boundary policy.

## Required behavior

- Make the post-commit cache effect durable/idempotent, for example with a transactionally written outbox/invalidation record drained with retry, or a cache design/version check that cannot outlive authoritative DB state.
- Continue across all requested job types and aggregate typed failures only after the full pass.
- Surface degraded recovery explicitly at the service/process boundary; do not annotate business recovery as J1/J7.
- Add real PGlite tests with a poisoned singleton in an earlier type and a recoverable row in a later type for startup and stale paths.
- Add a transient cache-failure test proving invalidation is retried after the job is already terminal.

Merged PR #17426 memoizes document requester authorization for an entire turn. The shared resolver is also called from document mutation paths, creating a same-turn stale-authorization window.

## Reproduction

Against exact PR head `6dcce4bbecd54f586f87654835b56930c12f0c93`:

1. Resolve a user's document requester snapshot during a read.
2. Revoke the user's room membership in the backing adapter.
3. Resolve again with the same message/turn context.
4. The resolver returns the original room set and the adapter records one membership read.

`updateDocument()` and `deleteDocument()` both consume this memoized resolver, so a revocation can be missed before mutation.

## Required repair

- Scope requester memoization to the concurrent DOCUMENTS provider read composition only.
- Resolve role and room membership uncached immediately before every update/delete authorization check.
- Add a real AgentRuntime + PGLite test covering read -> revoke -> update and delete in one turn; both mutations must fail.
- Cover rejected memo eviction and cross-turn isolation.

- Exact-parent/head adversarial PGLite logs.
- Focused document service/provider tests.

Related: #17426.30:T933,## Root cause (proven side-by-side)

`.github/workflows/cloud-tests.yml` (and sibling workflows) select runners with:

```yaml
runs-on: ${{ fromJSON(vars.HETZNER_FLEET_ONLINE == 'false' && '["ubuntu-24.04"]' || '["self-hosted","hetzner-robot"]') }}
```

The repo variable `HETZNER_FLEET_ONLINE` has been **`false` since 2026-07-05**. But **configuration variables are not delivered to workflow runs for pull requests from forks** — `vars.HETZNER_FLEET_ONLINE` evaluates as empty there, `'' == 'false'` is false, and the expression falls through to the fleet branch.

Observed at 2026-07-31 ~09:30Z, same workflow file, same commit window:

| PR | head repo | cloud lanes ran on |
|---|---|---|
| #17435 (same-repo branch) | elizaOS/eliza | `ubuntu-24.04` (GitHub-hosted) — [run 30620004875](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/30620004875) |
| #17349 (fork) | standujar/eliza | `self-hosted, hetzner-robot` — [run 30617154040](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/30617154040): eliza-prod-robot-5-r3, eliza-prod-robot-2-r1, eliza-prod-robot-4-r3, eliza-prod-robot-3-r2 |

## Impact

Every **fork** PR — i.e. the entire standujar/0xSolace swarm output — has its cloud lanes (and the other ~40 workflows using this expression) routed onto the offline, degraded fleet: docker.sock permission failures (prod-robot-2, prod-robot-5), `_diag/pages` corruption (robots 10/12/19/20), I/O starvation (10-min cache-key computations), ETXTBSY bun races, and round-number timeout kills. Full receipts in #17421. Same-repo PRs sail through on GitHub-hosted runners. This asymmetry is why the fork-PR queue has been stuck in rerun roulette all morning.

## Fix (one mechanical expression change, repo-wide)

Make the gate **fail-safe** for a missing variable — route to GitHub-hosted unless the fleet is explicitly online:

```yaml
runs-on: ${{ fromJSON(vars.HETZNER_FLEET_ONLINE != 'true' && '["ubuntu-24.04"]' || '["self-hosted","hetzner-robot"]') }}
```

`grep -rl "HETZNER_FLEET_ONLINE == 'false'" .github/workflows/ | xargs sed -i "s/HETZNER_FLEET_ONLINE == 'false'/HETZNER_FLEET_ONLINE != 'true'/g"` — then flip the variable to `true` only when the fleet is actually healthy.

Filed from the merge-queue sweep [pr-queue-review]; my token lacks workflow scope so this needs a workflow-scoped agent or maintainer. Relates to #17421.

32:T7a9,## Problem

Live production-path telemetry with the OpenAI-compatible Cerebras backend records a `MODEL_USED` event like:

```json
{
  "source": "openai",
  "provider": "openai",
  "type": "RESPONSE_HANDLER"
}
```

The concrete provider (`cerebras`) and concrete model (`gemma-4-31b`) are lost even though the handler already resolved both before the request. The agent usage capture then treats `type` as the model name. Pricing lookup cannot find `RESPONSE_HANDLER`, warns, and records the call cost as zero.

The same contract weakness exists in other emitters that omit concrete model/provider identity, while several plugins work around the incomplete core type using casts.

## Required fix

- make concrete provider and concrete model identity part of the core `ModelEventPayload` contract
- preserve the logical model slot separately (`type` / model label)
- have OpenAI-compatible mode report `cerebras`, `evolink`, or `openai` from the endpoint actually selected
- pass the resolved concrete model through text, embedding, and image-description usage emission
- update emitters that currently omit model/provider identity instead of allowing `type` to masquerade as a billable model
- remove unsafe event-payload casts made necessary by the incomplete core contract
- prove chat usage capture returns the concrete model/provider and pricing computes non-zero Cerebras cost
- run live Cerebras `gemma-4-31b` telemetry and verify there are no missing-price warnings

## Acceptance criteria

- `MODEL_USED.model` and `MODEL_USED.modelName` are the resolved provider model ID
- `MODEL_USED.provider` is the backend that served the call
- `MODEL_USED.type` remains the logical elizaOS model slot
- downstream usage aggregation never falls back from a missing model to a logical slot for successful hosted calls
- Cerebras Gemma usage produces a non-zero price-table cost
- all affected provider tests, agent usage tests, typechecks, and live evidence pass
33:T4e8,## Problem

`Scenario PR E2E` → **Zero-Key app browser view lifecycle** has been failing on `develop` across at least the last four runs (07-31T04:40, 05:01, 05:46, 06:35), and I can find no open issue for it. Two cases in `packages/app/test/ui-smoke/plugin-view-agent-bridge-inventory.spec.ts` fail:

**`:149` — plugin views expose concrete chat/voice-drivable controls through the agent bridge**
```
Error: Feed exposes spawn-agent through the agent bridge
```

**`:160` — registered app-shell plugin pages can be clicked through the bridge**
```
Error: page.evaluate: ViewCapabilityDeniedError: View "orchestrator" is not granted
capability "agent-click" (its surface manifest does not grant `agent-surface`)
```

## Why I filed rather than fixed

The second failure has two defensible readings and picking one is a product decision, not a mechanical fix:

1. **The view is wrong** — `orchestrator` is a registered app-shell page that a user can land on, so it should grant `agent-surface` and be agent-clickable like its peers. Fix: add the grant to its surface manifest.
2. **The test is wrong** — `orchestrator` deliberately withholds `agent-surface` (it is an operator/control surface where agent-driven clicking may be unsafe or meaningless), and the spec over-asserts by requiring *every* registered app-shell page to be bridge-clickable. Fix: scope the assertion to views that declare the capability, and state the exclusion.

Whoever owns the view-capability model should decide. If it is reading 1, note the first failure (`Feed exposes spawn-agent`) may share a root cause — both are the bridge inventory disagreeing with what surfaces actually grant.

## Notes for whoever picks it up

- The capability is enforced at the surface-manifest layer; `ViewCapabilityDeniedError` names both the missing capability (`agent-click`) and the missing grant (`agent-surface`), so the error is precise about what to change.
- `packages/agent/src/runtime/view-action-affinity.ts:248` documents the neighbouring design intent — a view with no `relatedActions` and no `ViewCapability` only warns, because "the universal agent-surface still reaches every control". That comment implies agent-surface is expected to be broadly available, which leans toward reading 1, but it is about a different code path and I would not treat it as decisive.
- Artifacts are attached to the failing runs: `test-results/plugin-view-agent-bridge-i-*/test-failed-1.png` for both cases.

Review verdict before merge: unsafe because production release validation could be bypassed.3d:T811,## Problem

PR #17392 merged as `5f5833c9dd28bfba984dc6dac51345e3e6470bf3` with destructive LifeOps definition access scoped by `agent_id + id`, but not `subject_type + subject_id`. The resolver lists all definitions for an agent and ambiguity output can disclose other subjects' titles; delete/update can target another owner or `agent_ops` definition.

The deeper defect combines four missing persistence invariants:

1. owner identity is not part of list/get/update/delete predicates;
2. fuzzy/planner text can authorize a destructive target rather than only nominate immutable IDs;
3. update/delete lack a revision predicate and affected-row check;
4. retry identity is an in-memory `WeakMap`, not a durable operation ledger.

Time-only edits can also rebuild a one-off reminder from today/tomorrow instead of preserving its existing local date, and timezone/window policy can diverge.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Enforce `agent_id`, `domain`, `subject_type`, and `subject_id` in repository-level list/get/update/delete operations.
- [ ] Require exactly one affected row and a monotonic revision or `expectedUpdatedAt`; stale mutations return a typed conflict for re-resolution.
- [ ] Planner/fuzzy text only nominates typed candidates; mutation occurs only from an immutable selected ID bound to owner/room/message clarification state.
- [ ] Add a durable operation ledger keyed by owner, stable message ID, and normalized request hash, transactionally coupled to mutation and receipt.
- [ ] Preserve existing zoned local date on time-only one-off edits and centrally enforce definition/window timezone equality.
- [ ] Add real PGlite tests with two owners under one agent, duplicate titles, cross-owner list/update/delete attempts, concurrent update/delete, restart/retry idempotency, and DST/timezone cases.
- [ ] Provide exact-head real-flow evidence; simulated/ineligible bundles do not satisfy acceptance.

## Defects

- [ ] A cloud-qualified request never silently degrades to local-device inventory.

- [ ] A sole complete/verified handled failure may terminal-gate while preserving `success: false`.
- [ ] Queued or multiple tool calls are never truncated by that terminal gate.

42:Tc70,## Problem

The open-PR audit found that the current document HTTP boundary is not a trustworthy authorization boundary:

- `plugins/plugin-documents/src/routes.ts#resolveRouteActor` trusts caller-supplied `x-eliza-entity-id` / `x-eliza-actor-entity-id` values.
- Omitting both headers defaults the caller to OWNER, so absence of authenticated identity is privilege escalation rather than rejection.
- Route search reconstructs a message-shaped identity instead of consuming a server-authenticated, process-private access context.
- Authorization must be applied before vector/BM25/hybrid ranking and top-K; fragment metadata alone must never widen the parent document policy.
- Multi-record document/fragment mutation and deletion need an atomic or CAS-protected contract so authorization cannot change between check and commit and partial fragment updates cannot survive.
- Pagination state must not be replayable across principals, filters, agents, or worlds.
- Client failures must remain a visible error state rather than a healthy empty document list.

PR #17269 contains some relevant tests and search changes, but its LifeOps audience boundary is weaker than the core gate already merged and the branch mixes 83 unrelated files. Extract and reimplement the document work as a focused correction from current `develop`; do not merge that aggregation.

Relates to #17269 and the document changes merged in #17266.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] HTTP document routes consume identity established by the authenticated server boundary; no public request header can mint OWNER, ADMIN, AGENT, or another user.
- [ ] Missing, malformed, conflicting, cross-agent, or cross-world identity fails closed with a structured 401/403 response.
- [ ] Internal callers pass a typed trusted `AccessContext`; serializable message metadata is never treated as authority.
- [ ] List, vector, keyword, and hybrid search authorize canonical parent documents before ranking/limits; fragments inherit the exact parent authorization revision and missing/malformed/stale metadata is denied.
- [ ] Create/update/delete of a document and its fragments is atomic or guarded by an exact revision CAS, including concurrent authorization changes and retry/idempotency behavior.
- [ ] Pagination uses stable snapshot semantics and an opaque context-bound cursor, or an equivalently proven non-replayable contract.
- [ ] The UI has distinct loading, designed-empty, unavailable, and transport/server-error states; no failed request is rendered as an empty store.
- [ ] Real AgentRuntime + authenticated HTTP + SQL/PGLite integration tests cover header forgery, missing identity, conflicting principals, cross-world access, parent/fragment mismatch, authorization changes during search and mutation, partial failure rollback, concurrency, pagination replay, and all search modes.
- [ ] Run package typechecks, lint, exact-head `bun run verify`, and the required app visual audit (at least five meaningful iterations if UI changes).
- [ ] Attach manually reviewed backend/frontend logs, domain rows, before/after desktop+mobile screenshots, and an MP4 walkthrough; mark genuinely inapplicable evidence explicitly.
43:T822,## Problem

Merged PR #17304 calls its conversation-recall envelope trusted, but the production boundary still accepts authority-bearing inputs from its caller and treats the presence of ordinary memory metadata as connector verification. In particular:

- `AccessContext` (including `isOwner`) and `destinationRoomId` are caller-supplied.
- `metadata[source].userId` is labeled `connector-verified` without an unforgeable attestation.
- source/account/scope fields can conflict and the first field silently wins.
- the adapter takes a global top-K before room/source/provenance/dedupe filtering, so closer ineligible rows can starve eligible results.
- Telegram message IDs are only chat-local, but the dedupe key omits the room.
- per-item withheld diagnostics expose account/platform identifiers.
- access-context lookup errors are swallowed into a fabricated low-authority context instead of surfacing observably.

The result is fail-closed in some happy-path tests, but the claimed trust and completeness do not hold at adversarial boundaries.

## Acceptance criteria

- Derive requester and destination only from process-bound trusted delivery-audience evidence minted for the exact runtime/turn; reject missing, stale, mismatched, or changed evidence.
- Resolve the requester access context internally; lookup failure must surface as a typed observable failure.
- Constrain the database vector scan by the attested room before ranking and refill a bounded candidate window honestly for source filtering, malformed records, and dedupe. Never report a truncated window as complete.
- Reject conflicting provenance fields and do not label structural metadata as connector-verified.
- Include room identity in the canonical dedupe key.
- Return aggregate denial diagnostics without memory/account/platform identifiers.
- Add adversarial exact-boundary tests plus a real PGLite + real MESSAGE-handler path proving forged caller authority, cross-room top-K starvation, source starvation, Telegram collisions, and withheld-ID leakage are impossible.

Corrective follow-up to #17304.44:T804,## Summary

The messaging-onboarding stack's credential preflight runs non-strict in CI (missing secrets print and pass), one required secret is missing from the preflight entirely, and every homepage channel test mocks the Cloud API — so none of the #17340/#17341 class of defects can be caught by CI today.

## 1. Preflight is non-strict in CI

`.github/workflows/cloud-gateway-discord.yml:74` and `cloud-gateway-webhook.yml:58` run `preflight:messaging-gateways` WITHOUT `--strict`; the `:strict` script exists in `packages/cloud/shared/package.json:35` and has no callers. A missing `ELIZA_APP_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` deploys green.

## 2. `ELIZA_APP_WEBHOOK_GATEWAY_SECRET` is not in the preflight at all

`_forward.ts:332-337` only stamps the forwarder-trust header when the secret is set — absence silently downgrades the gateway's "came from the BFF" proof. The preflight (`messaging-gateway-preflight.mjs`) checks neither `ELIZA_APP_WEBHOOK_GATEWAY_URL` nor `_SECRET`.

## 3. Channel test coverage cannot catch dead wiring

- `packages/homepage/tests/e2e/app-routes-flow.spec.ts:32-115` intercepts `https://www.elizacloud.ai/api/eliza-app/**` — fully mocked; no test exercises a real Cloud call.
- No test asserts the deep-link strings (`sms:+…`, `wa.me/…`, `t.me/…`, `discord.com/oauth2/authorize…`) — exactly what unset `VITE_*` env silently empties (#17340).
- No `?method=whatsapp` coverage; the landing platform switcher has no interaction test.
- Cloud side: `group-f-connectors.test.ts` is auth-boundary only; no test drives inbound webhook → onboarding → reply; the unlinked-Discord-DM scenario (design doc §540-547) does not exist — which is why #17341 shipped.

## Fix direction

Flip both workflows to `preflight:messaging-gateways:strict` once the secrets land (#17340 tracks them); add the gateway URL/SECRET pair to the preflight; add the deep-link string assertions (cheap, catches the whole #17340 class at build time); add the unlinked-DM scenario alongside the #17341 fix.

46:T749,## Summary

There is no way to bind a messaging-channel identity (an iMessage handle, a WhatsApp number) to an already-authenticated cloud account from the channel side — the structural half of "I want to use chat to send the agent messages but I need an app to see what is actually going on". Browser OAuth (`auth/telegram`, `auth/discord`) and gateway `trustedPlatformIdentity` are the only durable binding paths today.

## Gaps (all against origin/develop; the design of record is `packages/cloud/shared/docs/messaging-onboarding-gateway-design.md`)

1. **No `identity-link/start` / `identity-link/confirm` routes.** The design doc specifies them (§296-316); `packages/cloud/api/eliza-app/` has no `identity-link/` directory. Related: `POST /api/eliza-app/auth/whatsapp` accepts a bare `{ whatsapp_id }` with no Meta-signed proof — binding by claim alone, which is why the frontend never calls it (`loginWithWhatsApp` is defined in `auth-context.tsx` with zero call sites). The link-code flow is the prerequisite for wiring WhatsApp and iMessage auth at all.
2. **No negative-cache invalidation on link** (design §389-391): the webhook gateway caches identity-resolve misses in Redis; after a user completes OAuth in the browser, their very next DM can still fall into onboarding until the cache entry expires.
3. **Transcript handoff has no idempotency key** (design §352-354): `onboarding-chat.ts` stamps `handoffCopiedAt` without one, so a retried handoff can duplicate `/api/memory/remember` writes.

## Why now

#17336 makes the landing the front door; #17340/#17341 make the channels reachable. This issue is what makes the loop CLOSE: a user who onboarded via iMessage/WhatsApp can attach that handle to the account they later create (or already had) — otherwise the app and the chat remain two disconnected identities.

Refs #17323 [stan-cloud]
47:T99c,## Summary

A first-time user who DMs the Eliza Discord bot receives **no reply at all**. The Discord routing branch returns `{ handled: false }` on an unresolved sender and the gateway then logs-and-returns without replying — whereas the phone path onboards the same class of user. Verified by direct read.

## The asymmetry (both in agent-gateway-router.ts on origin/develop)

**Phone** — on an unresolved/unknown sender, runs onboarding (`agent-gateway-router.ts:713`, and again at `:733/:757` for the `unknown_owner` reasons):
```ts
const onboarding = await this.runOnboardingChat({
  message: args.body,
  platform: args.provider,
  platformUserId: args.from,
  sessionId: `platform:${args.provider}:${args.from}`,
  trustedPlatformIdentity: true,
});
return { handled: true, replyText: onboarding.reply, ... };
```

**Discord** — on `!resolved.target`, gives up (`agent-gateway-router.ts:639-646`):
```ts
if (!resolved.target) {
  return { handled: false, reason: resolved.reason, agentId: ..., userId: ... };
}
```
There is no `runOnboardingChat` call anywhere in the Discord branch (the four call sites are all phone). Downstream, `packages/cloud/services/gateway-discord/src/gateway-manager.ts` on `handled: false` emits a `logger.debug` and returns without sending — the user gets silence.

## Why it matters

This is the exact "onboard users through … discord" path. Discord is also the least stable of the four channels right now: of the three most recent Discord PRs, none merged — #17167 (outbound DM recipient registration, directly on the onboarding-DM send path) closed with CHANGES_REQUESTED and no landed fix, #17213 (group-room) is CHANGES_REQUESTED with schema risk, #17334 (button rendering) has red CI. So even once the router replies, the DM SEND path needs its own verification.

## Fix direction

Mirror the phone branch into the Discord branch at `agent-gateway-router.ts:639`: on `!resolved.target` with an onboarding-eligible reason, call `runOnboardingChat({ message, platform: "discord", platformUserId: sender.id, sessionId: \`platform:discord:${sender.id}\`, trustedPlatformIdentity: true })` and return `handled: true`. This is the open item in `packages/cloud/shared/docs/messaging-onboarding-gateway-design.md` (gap §81-84, rollout step 4) and needs the unlinked-DM scenario the design doc §540-547 asks for (`packages/test/scenarios/gateway/` currently covers only the linked case).

48:Td06,## Summary

The eliza.app experience being promoted by #17336 cannot onboard a single user through Telegram or Discord, and every channel's return link points at the wrong site. Three config-level defects, all verified by direct read at the cited lines; one PR fixes all three.

`.github/workflows/deploy-homepage.yml` (#17336 head) sets NONE of them: the `Build homepage` step runs `bun run build` with no channel env. Fix: add the four `VITE_*` values to the build step env in `deploy-homepage.yml` (and the Pages project's preview env). A runtime Pages var cannot work — Vite inlines at build.

## 2. The return link every bot sends points at the downloads site

`packages/cloud/api/wrangler.toml:162` and `:590` (both env blocks):
```toml
ELIZA_ONBOARDING_APP_URL = "https://elizaos-homepage.pages.dev"
```
`onboarding-chat.ts` builds the login link from `ELIZA_ONBOARDING_APP_URL` (it never reads `ELIZA_APP_URL`, which IS correctly `https://eliza.app` at `:161/:391/:589`). After #17336, the `elizaos-homepage` Pages project serves the OS/downloads page — so the `/get-started/?onboardingSession=…` link texted to every Telegram/WhatsApp/iMessage user resolves to the wrong site.

Fix: `ELIZA_ONBOARDING_APP_URL = "https://eliza.app"` in both blocks. Consider collapsing onto `ELIZA_APP_URL` to remove the duplicate-var trap.

## 3. Staging has no ELIZA_ONBOARDING_APP_URL at all

The staging block sets `ELIZA_APP_URL` but not `ELIZA_ONBOARDING_APP_URL` → the fallback chain lands on `https://staging.elizacloud.ai/get-started/…`, a route that does not exist on the Cloud console. Staging channel onboarding is untestable end-to-end until the var exists there.

## Ops sibling (not a code change, tracked here for completeness)

`CEREBRAS_API_KEY` is a Worker secret (wrangler secret, per env — the required-secrets manifest comments it at `packages/cloud/api/wrangler.toml:279`). Without it, channel onboarding silently degrades to canned replies (`onboarding-chat.ts` returns null client → static fallback) and the browser post-login chat hard-fails disguised as "your space is still warming up" (`provisioning-agent-chat.ts:74-76` throw, swallowed by the hook). # Risks

Large. This adds a public skill and Vite site and changes repository-wide
evidence, attribution, and AI-workflow policy. The main risks are incomplete
GitHub pagination, incorrect scoring, unsafe skill installation, policy false
positives, and deploying bytes that differ from the verified build.

The implementation fails closed on incomplete data, validates the skill archive
and checksum, evaluates PR policy from the trusted base revision, limits
automation permissions, and binds deployment verification to the tested SHA.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds the bundled `contribute-to-eliza` skill for finishing issues and
  reviewing PRs with claims, tests, evidence, validation, and exact model
  disclosure.
- Adds `packages/eliza-computer`, the Vite site for `eliza.army`, with a GitHub
  snapshot, contribution leaderboard, work queue, verified skill download, and
  public payout-address instructions.
- States the offer precisely: a $10,000 monthly USDC pool for accepted elizaOS
  contributions. Leaderboard points do not determine payouts.
- Adds attribution and evidence checks for PRs, issue comments, reviews, and
  repository AI workflows.
- Adds exact-artifact Cloudflare Pages CI/CD with a protected production
  environment.

## What kind of change is this?

Feature, with supporting policy, security, documentation, CI, and tests.

# Documentation

Updated `CONTRIBUTING.md`, issue and PR templates, workflow documentation,
package product/design/deployment guides, the package map, and the skill's
canonical references.

# Risks

Medium-to-large in blast radius, risk-reducing in direction. This changes a production deploy path under `.github/workflows`, so it needs human review and must not be auto-merged. It does not weaken any existing check; every change either tightens a boundary or holds it. The operational surface that changes is the release runbook (operators now dispatch from `develop` with a `candidate_run_id` instead of dispatching from the candidate branch), so a release operator should confirm that flow before the next promotion.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

### [P1] Candidate-controlled workflow code entered the production-secret job

A manually selected candidate SHA supplied `.github/workflows/eliza-computer.yml` and every deployment script, and the job then entered the production environment and received Cloudflare credentials. The admission check ran *inside* the candidate-controlled definition, so the candidate could modify or bypass the check meant to gate it. Human environment approval reduced likelihood but was not a code-enforced trust boundary.

The deploy job now:

- checks out `refs/heads/develop` and verifies through the API that the checkout is current `origin/develop`;
- rejects any `production-candidate` dispatch from a ref other than `develop`;
- accepts a candidate's build **by run identity** through a new `candidate_run_id` input, replacing `candidate_pr`.

The candidate contributes exactly one thing: the immutable artifact built by the secretless `quality` job. The protected admission step requires that run to be completed, successful, same-repository, not `pull_request`/`pull_request_target`, and a run of *this* workflow file, and requires its head SHA to be the current head of an open same-repository PR into `develop` that is zero commits behind a freshly fetched `origin/develop`. Deploy metadata, byte verification, and the Cloudflare commit hash all bind to the admitted bundle SHA.

Side benefit: because a candidate branch is never dispatched directly, the `eliza-army-production` deployment-branch allowlist keeps `develop` as its only entry and no longer needs the temporary-allowlist step the old runbook described.

### [P1] Production resolved Wrangler outside `bun.lock`

The job ran `bunx wrangler@4.100.0` *after* entering the production environment, so the secret-bearing lane could re-resolve executable content from a registry at deploy time. Per the correction on #17424, wrangler was already in `bun.lock` via the root override; the defect was that the job never installed from the lock and never invoked the installed binary.

Now: `bun install --frozen-lockfile --ignore-scripts --filter @elizaos/eliza-computer`, then execute `packages/eliza-computer/node_modules/.bin/wrangler` for both the project-create and deploy paths. A preceding step asserts the binary reports the pinned version and fails **before** any credential is used. `wrangler@4.100.0` becomes an explicit `@elizaos/eliza-computer` devDependency matching the existing root override, which adds exactly one line to `bun.lock`.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (security boundary repair in CI/CD), plus the documentation that describes the corrected release procedure.

## Two-project topology (re: the #17413 "packages/homepage/dist" note)

`eliza.army` and `eliza.app` are **separate** Cloudflare Pages projects and this deploy does not fight the existing homepage topology:

- `eliza-computer.yml` → project `eliza-computer` → `eliza.army`, built from `packages/eliza-computer` (standalone Vite site, `wrangler.toml` owns `pages_build_output_dir = "./dist"`).
- `deploy-homepage.yml` → project `eliza-app-home` → `eliza.app`, built from `packages/homepage`.

Their `paths:` filters are disjoint, so neither can preempt or overwrite the other. Documented in `packages/eliza-computer/AGENTS.md` so the two projects are not conflated later.

# Documentation changes needed?

Done in this PR. `packages/eliza-computer/AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` carry the corrected release runbook and the two-project topology; `.github/workflows/CHANGELOG.md` records the CI policy change.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`.github/workflows/eliza-computer.yml`, the `deploy:` job. Read it as a security boundary: confirm nothing executed in that job originates from the candidate ref, and that no credential is referenced before the admission and wrangler-version steps have passed. Then read `packages/eliza-computer/scripts/deployment-contract.test.mjs`, which is what keeps those properties from regressing.

- `packages/eliza-computer` suite: **99/99 pass** (`deployment-contract.test.mjs` 9/9)
- `tsc -b` clean; `biome check .` clean across 32 files

# Deploy Notes

The release runbook changes. Operators no longer dispatch from the candidate branch; they dispatch from `develop` with `release_mode=production-candidate` and `candidate_run_id` set to the candidate's already-finished `quality` run. `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` are updated to match. ## What changed

This integrates the reviewed latency/streaming work as one rebased branch on current `develop`:

- removes guessed chat, message-service, Discord, desktop proxy, and orchestrator generation deadlines;
- propagates caller/lifecycle cancellation through chat routes, message dispatch, model streaming, native bridges, local inference, Discord delivery, desktop IPC, Android and iOS;
- replaces orchestrator polling with ready/reconnected events and awaited shutdown drains;
- preserves upstream Discord exact-delivery receipts and audience attestation;
- parallelizes dependency-safe plugin/provider/database work, batches relationship entity reads, removes duplicate relationship ownership, and coalesces document authorization within the turn-owned memo;
- makes embedding metadata declarative rather than fabricating probe vectors;
- accelerates and owns Android Kokoro PCM/TTS streaming;
- keeps performance CI informational and removes threshold/ratchet/changed-source-test gating;
- adds real provider, chat-path, browser/PGLite, native, cancellation, and lifecycle telemetry/tests.

4c:Tfb3,Extends the abuse-aware scenario coverage that landed for the co-parent packs to every other persona pack.

## Why

An edge-case audit across all 14 persona catalogs found the co-parenting pack was the **only** one with meaningful coverage of the five dimensions real users actually produce. Most packs scored zero on all five:

| pack | rows | ambiguity | contradiction | privacy probe | injection | approval pressure |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| executive-assistant-transcript | 21 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| adhd-follow-through | 24 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| shift-rotation | 22 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| rupture-repair | 10 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| overdue-comms-apology | 10 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| co-parenting (already done) | 21 | 1 | 1 | 4 | 1 | 2 |

The corpus tested happy paths, not the behaviour that actually breaks assistants.

## What

**60 new rows across 12 packs**, five dimensions each: an ambiguous referent needing clarification, a mid-conversation self-contradiction that must supersede rather than duplicate, a third-party disclosure probe, instructions embedded in untrusted forwarded content that must be treated as data, and pressure to skip an outbound approval the agent must hold.

Each is grounded in that persona's own life rather than transplanted from the co-parent pack — a shift worker's ambiguity is a timezone-or-shift-boundary problem ("tomorrow" on a night shift), an ADHD persona's is two open loops, a caregiver's is which person is being supported, an EA's is which meeting or attendee.

## Grading

Semantics are judged by LLM rubrics that enumerate failure modes **including paraphrase**; deterministic matching is confined to named canary tokens and false-completion literals. For example the medical-probe rubric fails a response that "reveals **or hints at** the diagnosis" or "treats 'she wants to help' as consent" — a keyword list cannot distinguish those from a legitimate supportive reply, and pinning tone to string membership produces false verdicts in both directions.

Every new turn carries a `responseJudge`; every scenario an end-to-end `judgeRubric`. Structural gates stay deterministic where an LLM cannot replace them: `plannerExcludes`, `expectNoExternalSendDispatch`, `messageDelivered: false`, `memoryWriteOccurred`.

## Verification

- scenario-runner static guards (they walk every scenario file): **24/24 passing**
- persona catalog coverage gate: **exit 0**, corpus 344 → **414 authored rows**

Rows are registered `authored`, **not** verified — this changes nothing about live-provider evidence, and no pack's verified count moves.

4e:T1333,## P4 multiplayer coordination: group-room primitive in the Discord connector

Multiple humans + multiple agents in one room currently produce three structural failures: two agents answering the same human message, agents replying to superseded ("stale") human edges, and bot-to-bot reply loops. The Sol+Vera coordination protocol solved this behaviorally; this PR productizes it as a **runtime primitive** in `plugins/plugin-discord`, opt-in via `DISCORD_GROUP_COORDINATION_ENABLED` (default **off** — zero behavior change for existing deployments).

**Scope note:** deliberately does NOT touch outbound DM participant registration (#17167, @nubs's lane).

### What it does

New module `group-coordination.ts`, wired into `MessageManager.handleMessage` + the gateway `messageCreate` listener:

1. **One active speaker per human edge.** Before committing a model turn, an agent must win a durable *speaker lease* keyed `(channelId, edgeMessageId)`. The lease row id uses `stringToUuid` (agent-**independent**, unlike `createUniqueUuid`) so all agents sharing a coordination store contend on the same row. The claim is the adapter's existing atomic `onConflictDoNothing` insert followed by a read-back — the read-back decides the winner, both racers converge on the same holder. No CAS, no advisory locks, no new schema.
2. **Safe expiry/reclaim.** Leases carry `expiresAt`. A crashed holder's edge is reclaimed at **generation+1** (another atomic unique-row insert; expired rows are never mutated). A revived stale holder fails `verifySpeakerLease` (expired / superseded / not-holder) and **aborts before the third-party send** and before persisting any reply.
3. **Latest-human-edge-wins.** The gateway records each human (non-bot) message as the channel's edge. A claimed turn superseded by a newer human edge aborts at claim time *and* again pre-send. Explicit addressing (mention/reply-to-bot) overrides staleness — explicitly addressed work is never dropped — and a coalesced batch containing the latest edge stays current, so bursts coalesce per policy without dropping addressed work.
4. **Bot-to-bot loop prevention.** Bot messages never advance the human edge. A bot-authored message earns a reply only when it explicitly addresses this bot AND the per-channel consecutive bot-reply budget (`DISCORD_BOT_REPLY_BUDGET`, default 1, reset on every human message) is not exhausted. Two mutually-mentioning bots structurally stall after the budget until a human speaks.
5. **Auditable receipts.** Every decision (lease-claim won/lost/reclaimed/renewed, stale-edge abort, lost-lease abort, bot-loop suppression) persists a row in `discord_coordination_receipts` scoped to the room, with edge id, outcome, generation, and holder.

Retry/redelivery cannot double-send: the lease claim is idempotent per holder ("renewed"), and the existing durable-turn machine (#16696 lineage) reconciles a redelivery to REPLIED from the persisted reply memory.

### Tests (all green at exact head `eb5a727a7f3`)

- **`__tests__/group-coordination.test.ts`** (18) — protocol unit suite: concurrent claim race → one winner + loser observes winner; idempotent retry; expiry → generation+1 reclaim; two successors race an expired lease → one reclaimer; superseded verification; edge currency incl. explicit-mention override, coalesced-batch currency, out-of-order monotonic guard; bot budget + human-reset; receipt persistence + store-failure safety; config defaults.
- **`__tests__/messages-group-coordination.test.ts`** (6) — two REAL `MessageManager`s (two agents) share one store and race the same human message → exactly one third-party send, both leave lease receipts agreeing on the winner; newer human edge mid-generation → pre-send abort with receipt; explicit mention still sends; unaddressed bot message suppressed (ingested, receipted); addressed bot reply honors budget then exhausts; restart redelivery is a no-op (no double send).
- **`test/group-coordination-pglite.test.ts`** (5) — real `AgentRuntime` + in-process **PGlite** via the repo's shared test-runtime helper: the claim race, idempotent retry, crash→expiry→g+1 reclaim, revived-holder superseded abort, and receipt rows proven against the actual plugin-sql `onConflictDoNothing` seam (the atomicity the protocol depends on).
- Full `plugin-discord` unit suite: **66 files / 501 tests pass** including the new ones; durable-turn, inbound-idempotency, staleness, coalesce suites untouched-green. Biome clean; `tsc` introduces no new errors in touched files.

### Not in scope

- No prod deploy, no workflow edits.
- No changes to #17167 (outbound DM participant registration) or its branch.
- Cross-process lease contention through one shared DB is exercised via two holder identities on one PGlite store; a multi-runtime docker-compose soak (the P4 "week in a room with Shadow+Shaw+Nubs" green bar) is the follow-up dogfood step.

# Risks

**Medium.** This is an authorization boundary, so both directions matter.

- **Fail-closed risk (mitigated):** the schema documents `groupPolicy: "allowlist"` as the default. Applying that literally would silently mute every existing env-only deployment that never wrote a policy. Resolution: `allowlist` applies as soon as *any* channel config exists; a deployment with no channel config at all resolves to `open`, and the resolution source is logged (`groupPolicySource: implicit-open|implicit-allowlist|explicit`). Same reasoning for DMs: no `dm` block at all means `legacy` (historical always-allowed).
- **New startup failures (intended):** ambiguous/unresolvable/deactivated identifiers and unhonorable config now throw during `startAccount`. This is the point of the change — an operator is entitled to have their policy enforced or to be told loudly that it is not — but it does mean a config that previously "worked" (by being ignored) can now fail to boot. Every message names the offending key and the fix.
- **`dm.policy="pairing"` now fails closed.** It is the schema default *once a `dm` block exists*, and this connector implements no pairing handshake. Admitting every DM instead would be the silent-acceptance bug again. Logged loudly at startup with the two working alternatives.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`SlackConfigSchema` has long accepted a rich policy surface — per-channel `enabled`/`requireMention`/`users`/`allowBots`, account `groupPolicy`, and a `dm` block — while `SlackService` gated purely on the global `SLACK_SHOULD_RESPOND_ONLY_TO_MENTIONS` env flag and the `SLACK_CHANNEL_IDS` allowlist. **Config that looked applied was silently dropped**, which is the worst possible failure mode for an authorization boundary.

The review established the defect is deeper than the gate helper, so this replaces the helper with one authoritative policy model.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (security-relevant: an authorization boundary that did not exist).

# Blocker-by-blocker response

### [P0-1] Config never reaches the gate — `c764af80f04`

Two independent drops, both fixed:

1. `buildCharacterFromConfig()` projected only the three Slack token env fields, so `connectors.slack` never landed on `character.settings.slack`. Now the non-credential **policy** object is projected as a plain setting (credentials keep flowing through `secrets`, redactable). Agent-entry `settings.slack` still wins so injected sandbox characters override file config.
2. `getMultiAccountConfig()` (accounts.ts) hand-picked 4 keys and discarded the rest. `SlackConfigSchema` is literally `SlackAccountSchema.extend({accounts})` — **the top level IS an account config** — so the object is now passed through whole, and `SlackMultiAccountConfig extends SlackAccountConfig` to match the schema's real shape.

Your exact reproduction is now a test. Controls assert the projection, the account resolution, **and** the enforcement at the registered Bolt handler, from a persisted `ElizaConfig`.

### [P0-2] Structured allowlists fail open — `1e27ba37ec6`

Root fix: **names are resolved to immutable IDs at startup**, once, before any handler is registered. Event-time authorization is an exact opaque-string comparison that never depends on a warm cache. Consequently:

- name-only records no longer produce an empty (therefore allow-all) allowlist;
- a name-keyed `enabled:false` is enforced on the **first** event;
- ambiguous names and unresolvable **restricted** entries **fail startup**;
- `handleMemberJoinedChannel` now **obeys** `groupPolicy` — a join no longer unions into admission, so invite-the-bot is no longer an allowlist bypass;
- `groupPolicy` is enforced (see Risks for the legacy-deployment carve-out), and `allowlist` with an empty allowlist fails startup rather than admitting nothing.

A pure-ID config costs **zero** startup API calls (asserted).

### [P1-3] User allowlist contract — `1e27ba37ec6`

Handlers compare the sender's opaque ID against IDs resolved at startup, so configured handles finally match. Ambiguity, unresolvable entries, and **deactivated** users all fail startup (a freed handle can be reused by another account — that is a live privilege-transfer bug). Per your Slack link, ID prefix shape is used **only** to decide whether a config key is a literal identifier or a name needing resolution; every authorization comparison is exact-match against an API-returned ID.

**`users: []` is now an EXPLICIT empty allowlist that denies everyone** — documented, warned at startup, and tested. Treating it as "no policy" was a fail-open.

### [P1-4] Event classification — `1e27ba37ec6`

`classifySlackEvent()` explicitly separates `im` / `app_home` / `mpim` / `channel` / private-channel. App Home traffic arrives on `D` channel IDs and was matching `channels["*"]`, so a channel-scoped `enabled:false` silently killed DMs. MPIM now runs on `dm.groupEnabled`/`dm.groupChannels` instead of channel policy. Unclassifiable events fail closed.

**DM policy is implemented here** (folding in the old slice 2): `open`/`allowlist`/`disabled` enforced; `pairing` fails closed with a loud startup warning, documented in Risks.

### [P1-5] One authoritative typed policy resolver — `1e27ba37ec6`

`allowlist.ts` is deleted; `policy.ts` owns account/channel/DM/event context in one resolver, reached from both handlers through a single call site so the two paths cannot drift.

`SlackChannelConfig` now mirrors `SlackChannelSchema` **exactly**: `tools`/`toolsBySender` added, invented `replyToMode` removed (the `.strict()` schema never accepted it, so it could not arrive through the production path), `allowBots` consumed. **Config the service cannot honor now FAILS configuration**: per-channel `tools`/`toolsBySender` and `mode:"http"` throw at startup; inert-but-harmless keys raise startup warnings naming exactly what has no effect.

### [P1-6] Evidence — partially complete, **live Slack round trip still needs a human**

Fully done, and the fake-Bolt criticism is addressed: **no test hand-constructs `ResolvedSlackAccount` any more.** `test-harness.ts` deliberately offers no way to inject one, because that shortcut is precisely how a gate receiving no configuration passed its own suite. Every gating test now drives the real chain: persisted `ElizaConfig` to real `buildCharacterFromConfig()` to `character.settings.slack` to real `resolveSlackAccount()` to real `resolveSlackAccountPolicy()` to real `registerEventHandlers()` to the captured Bolt callbacks.

**Not done, and I am not going to claim otherwise:** a real/sandbox Slack round trip with raw inbound payloads, outbound API responses, and reviewed logs. I cannot create a Slack workspace or issue `xoxb-`/`xapp-` tokens from this environment. Exactly what a human needs to do is listed under **Known gaps** below.

### [P0-3] Credentials crossed the secret boundary — `0dca2afeb39`

Your exact-head blocker, and you were right. The previous head assigned the whole canonical `connectors.slack` object to `character.settings.slack`. `SlackAccountSchema` declares `botToken` / `appToken` / `userToken` / `signingSecret` at the base level **and inside every `accounts.<id>` entry**, so all of them left the `settings.secrets` boundary the redactor scans and became ordinary settings — and the assignment shared the persisted object by reference.

Root fix rather than a strip-list:

- `projectConnectorPolicy()` returns a typed credential-free **view**: a deep clone (no shared structure with the persisted config) with every credential-classified key removed **at any depth**. Classification reuses the repo's own `isSensitiveConfigKey`, the same predicate driving config-API redaction, so "what is a credential" has one definition. It is deny-by-predicate, so a credential field added to the schema later is excluded by default instead of leaking.
- The stripped credentials are not lost. Base-level credentials (which every account inherits, since `SlackConfigSchema` is `SlackAccountSchema.extend({accounts})`) and per-account credentials are hoisted to deterministic keys on the **secret** path. Per-account credentials previously had no secret lane at all — that is what made plain-settings projection look necessary for multi-account.
- Key derivation lives once in `@elizaos/core` (`connectorAccountCredentialSettingKey` / `connectorBaseCredentialSettingKey`) so the projecting side and the consuming plugin cannot drift. One key per credential deliberately: the redactor's known-secret set is built from secret *values*, so a single packed JSON blob would only redact as a whole and let individual tokens through.
- `resolveSlackAccount()` reads credentials from the secret path, per-account overriding base, with the flat `SLACK_*` env lane still last and still default-account-only.

Exact-schema tests enumerate the live `SlackAccountSchema` / `SlackConfigSchema` shapes rather than a hand-copied list, and assert base and per-account credential fields never appear in plain character settings (**by key and by value**) while every policy field still reaches the Slack resolver. The independent credential derivation is type-aware, so `userTokenReadOnly` — a boolean policy flag that matches a naive `/token/` name test — is correctly not treated as a credential.

### Typecheck claim correction

You were right. The "107 baseline + 2 new errors" figure in the old body was an unbuilt-core artifact, not a real result. With core built first (`bun run --cwd packages/core build`), `bun run --cwd plugins/plugin-slack typecheck` **passes with zero errors**, and I fixed the two genuine type defects this rework introduced rather than reporting around them.

# Documentation changes needed?

No repo docs change. Behaviour that operators must know about is surfaced **at runtime**, which is more reliable than a doc: startup logs the resolved `groupPolicy` + its source, and warns by name for every configured key that has no effect.
